=== Schema for Error Log 1 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - The agent selects an option that is tangentially related to the correct answer but does not directly address the core reason or benefit mentioned in the context.
  - The agent provides a rationale that is overly focused on a single aspect of the technology, which is not the most compelling or relevant reason for its adoption as per the given context.
  - The agent's choice and rationale are based on a misunderstanding or misinterpretation of the key benefits or features of the technology in question.

- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - The agent's response is concise and does not explore multiple aspects of the given options.
  - The agent jumps to a conclusion without considering all the available information or options.
  - The agent uses terms that are not explicitly mentioned or are only loosely related to the core issue (e.g., "scientific advancement" instead of "environmentally friendly" when the context emphasizes environmental benefits).

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Typical Contextual Conditions:**
  - The question involves a comparison between two technologies or products, where one has a clear environmental or social benefit.
  - The context provides specific reasons or benefits for choosing one technology over the other, but the agent focuses on a general or less relevant aspect.
  - The options include a mix of technical features, general benefits, and irrelevant or false statements.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The agent is presented with a task that requires evaluating specific reasons or benefits.
  - The agent does not break down the task into manageable steps or gather additional information.
  - The agent quickly decides on an option and provides a rationale that aligns more with a general perception rather than the specific context provided.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine if the Error is Present:**
  - Does the selected answer directly address the core reason or benefit mentioned in the context?
  - Are there any specific phrases in the context that the agent's rationale should have focused on?
  - Is the agent's rationale based on a broad or general statement rather than the specific details provided?

- **Analytical Framework to Identify the Error Pattern:**
  - **Context Relevance Check:** Evaluate whether the agent's chosen answer and rationale are directly relevant to the specific context and details provided in the task.
  - **Option Analysis:** Compare the agent's chosen option with each of the other options to ensure that the most compelling and contextually appropriate reason is selected.
  - **Rationale Depth Assessment:** Assess whether the agent's rationale is shallow or lacks depth, considering only surface-level or general aspects of the technology rather than the specific benefits or reasons mentioned.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns Serving as Reliable Indicators:**
  - **Overgeneralization:** Phrases like "it's a scientific advancement," "it's a new technology," or "it's a modern solution" without linking them to the specific benefits or reasons in the context.
  - **Feature Focus:** Responses that focus on technical features (e.g., "they store energy in batteries") without explaining why these features are important or beneficial.
  - **Misalignment with Context:** Rationales that do not mention key phrases from the context, such as "no carbon monoxide," "environmentally friendly," or "reducing carbon footprint."
  - **Hasty Conclusion:** Immediate selection of an answer without a step-by-step analysis or consideration of all options.

By using this template, one can systematically identify when an agent is making similar errors in future conversations, particularly in tasks that require a nuanced understanding of the context and a detailed evaluation of multiple options.

=== Schema for Error Log 2 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Misinterpretation of Scientific Mechanisms**: The agent incorrectly interprets the underlying scientific mechanism, leading to a choice that contradicts well-established principles (e.g., choosing proton transfer over electron transfer in a scenario where electron transfer is the primary mechanism).
- **Overcomplication of Simple Concepts**: The agent introduces complex or nuanced scientific explanations that are not typically required or expected for the level of the question (e.g., detailed surface chemistry mechanisms for a basic physics question).
- **Confusion Between Similar Scientific Terms**: The agent confuses similar scientific terms or processes, such as mixing up protons and electrons, or ion transfer and electron transfer.
- **Premature Task Completion**: The agent completes the task and provides a final answer without adequately breaking down the task or gathering necessary information, relying instead on a superficial or incorrect understanding.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Initial Assessment Step**: This error occurs during the initial assessment of the task, before any capabilities are engaged to gather additional information.
- **Lack of Simplified Explanations**: The agent does not consider or mention the simplified, commonly accepted explanation (e.g., basic electron transfer in static electricity) that is suitable for the context of the question.
- **Overreliance on Nuanced Science**: The agent attempts to use more advanced or nuanced scientific concepts without verifying if these are relevant or necessary for the question at hand.
- **Absence of Step-by-Step Breakdown**: The task is not broken down into smaller, manageable steps, and the agent jumps directly to a conclusion based on a flawed initial understanding.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine Error Presence**:
  - Is the chosen answer consistent with the basic scientific principles typically taught at the level of the question?
  - Does the agent's explanation introduce concepts that are more complex than necessary for the context?
  - Is there a clear and logical breakdown of the task into steps, or does the agent provide a premature conclusion?
  - Does the agent confuse or mix up fundamental scientific terms (e.g., protons with electrons)?

- **Analytical Framework for Identifying Error Pattern**:
  - **Evaluate the Complexity of the Explanation**: Assess whether the explanation aligns with the expected level of complexity for the question. For basic questions, overly complex explanations can be a red flag.
  - **Check for Consistency with Standard Models**: Verify if the agent's response is consistent with standard scientific models and principles that are widely accepted and taught.
  - **Look for Step-by-Step Reasoning**: Ensure that the agent's response includes a step-by-step breakdown of the reasoning process. The absence of this can indicate a lack of thorough analysis.
  - **Cross-Reference with Educational Materials**: Compare the agent's answer with standard educational materials to check for alignment with typical teaching points.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns as Indicators**:
  - "The correct answer is [option] because of [complex scientific mechanism] rather than [simpler, commonly accepted mechanism]."
  - "While many textbook explanations simplify to [common mechanism], [nuanced or advanced mechanism] is actually more accurate."
  - "This is the initial assessment step, and no previous specialist output is available."
  - "The task can be completed without further information or analysis."
  - "The rod ending up negative can be described as [confused or overcomplicated mechanism]."

This template can be used to recognize when an agent is making similar errors in future conversations by looking for these distinctive patterns, contextual conditions, and key phrases.

=== Schema for Error Log 3 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - **Premature Task Completion:** The agent declares the task complete without engaging in a proper analysis or information gathering process.
  - **Incorrect Selection of Answer:** The agent selects an answer that is factually incorrect or only partially correct, often based on a superficial understanding of the options provided.
  - **Lack of Step-by-Step Breakdown:** The agent fails to break down the task into manageable steps, leading to an incomplete or inaccurate analysis.
  - **Ignoring Available Capabilities:** The agent does not utilize available capabilities (like WebSurfer or FileSurfer) to gather necessary information to make an informed decision.

- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - **Rushed Response:** The agent provides a quick and definitive answer without showing any signs of deliberation or research.
  - **Confidence in Incorrect Information:** The agent expresses high confidence in an answer that contradicts known facts or common knowledge.
  - **Lack of Evidence:** The agent's response lacks supporting evidence or references to credible sources.
  - **Logical Inconsistencies:** The agent's reasoning contains logical flaws or does not align with the context of the question.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - **Complex Multiple Choice Questions:** The error occurs in a scenario where the question is a multiple-choice format, requiring a nuanced understanding of the options.
  - **Scientific or Technical Topics:** The question involves a scientific or technical topic where specific knowledge is crucial.
  - **First Action Scenario:** The error is often identified in the first action taken by the agent, indicating a lack of preliminary research or analysis.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - **Direct Task Assignment:** The user directly assigns a task to the agent without providing additional context or resources.
  - **Immediate Task Completion:** The agent immediately completes the task without consulting other agents or using available capabilities.
  - **No Follow-Up Questions:** The agent does not ask clarifying questions or seek additional information, even when the user's initial message suggests complexity or ambiguity.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine Error Presence:**
  - **"Can you explain how you arrived at this answer?"** 
  - **"Did you use any specific resources or capabilities to verify your answer?"**
  - **"Are you familiar with the latest research or data on this topic?"**
  - **"Could you elaborate on the characteristics of the troposphere that led you to choose this option?"**

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Evaluate Thoroughness:** Assess whether the agent has broken down the task into smaller, manageable steps and has considered all options.
  - **Check for Research:** Verify if the agent has used any of the available capabilities to gather information, such as WebSurfer or FileSurfer.
  - **Cross-Reference Facts:** Compare the agent's answer and reasoning with known scientific facts or standard educational material.
  - **Assess Logical Consistency:** Ensure that the agent's reasoning is logically sound and aligns with the context of the question.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **"TASK_COMPLETE" without prior steps:** This phrase, when used prematurely, is a strong indicator that the agent has not adequately analyzed the task.
  - **"Final Answer: [Option]"** immediately after receiving the question, without any intermediate steps or evidence.
  - **"Based on my understanding..."** followed by a brief and possibly incorrect explanation, suggesting a lack of research.
  - **"The [layer/phenomenon] is known for..."** when the statement is not fully accurate or is based on outdated information.

### Application of the Template:
- **When to Apply:**
  - Use this template when a task involves a complex multiple-choice question, especially in scientific or technical domains.
  - Apply the template if the agent's response seems overly confident or lacks detailed explanation and evidence.
  - Consider the template if the agent declares the task complete in the first step without utilizing available capabilities.

- **How to Apply:**
  - **Question the Process:** Ask the agent to describe their thought process and the steps they took to arrive at their answer.
  - **Request Evidence:** Inquire about the sources or data used to support their conclusion.
  - **Challenge the Answer:** Pose follow-up questions that test the depth and accuracy of their knowledge.
  - **Review for Logical Flaws:** Carefully examine the agent's reasoning for any logical inconsistencies or gaps in understanding.

By using this template, you can systematically identify when an agent might be making a similar error in future conversations, particularly in scenarios involving complex, fact-based questions.

=== Schema for Error Log 4 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Error Identification in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Misinterpretation of Biological Mechanisms:** The agent demonstrates a misunderstanding of how a biological mechanism (in this case, ADH) functions, leading to an incorrect conclusion.
- **Premature Task Completion:** The agent declares the task complete and provides a final answer without engaging any of the available specialists or conducting a systematic analysis.
- **Incorrect Logical Flow:** The agent's reasoning contains logical inconsistencies. For example, it states that "with too little water, kidneys excrete ADH, lowering effective ADH at the collecting duct," which is contradictory to the known function of ADH.
- **Confusion Between Similar Concepts:** The agent confuses the effects of ADH with the opposite expected outcome (increased urine volume instead of decreased).

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Task Involves Complex Biological Processes:** The task requires understanding of a complex biological process, which the agent may not fully grasp or may have incorrect prior knowledge about.
- **Lack of Initial Information Gathering:** The error occurs in the first step of the conversation, indicating that the agent did not properly gather or verify initial information before making a decision.
- **Absence of Specialist Engagement:** The agent did not utilize any of the available specialists (WebSurfer, FileSurfer, Coder, CodeExecutor) to validate its understanding or to break down the task into more manageable steps.
- **Rushed or Oversimplified Analysis:** The agent's response is overly simplified, suggesting a lack of thorough analysis of the biological process involved.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions:**
  - "Can you explain the function of ADH in the body?"
  - "What happens when ADH levels are high versus low?"
  - "How does the body typically respond to low water levels?"
  - "Did you consult any specialists or resources before reaching your conclusion?"
  - "Can you break down the steps that led to your final answer?"

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Step-by-Step Verification:** Check if the agent has broken down the task into logical steps and verified each step with appropriate data or resources.
  - **Consistency in Mechanism Explanation:** Assess whether the agent's explanation of biological mechanisms is consistent with established scientific knowledge.
  - **Utilization of Specialists:** Determine if the agent has engaged the available specialists to gather necessary information or to perform a deeper analysis.
  - **Cross-Referencing Answers:** Compare the agent's final answer with known facts or previous research to identify discrepancies.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - "With too little water, [organ] excretes [hormone]." (Look for confusion in the organ or hormone mentioned)
  - "Lower [hormone] → [effect]." (Identify if the agent incorrectly assumes the effect of lower hormone levels)
  - "Therefore, the correct outcome is:" (Evaluate if the conclusion logically follows from the premises)
  - "No previous specialist output yet." (Check if the agent has made a decision without consulting specialists)
  - "TASK_COMPLETE" or "Final Answer:" (Verify if these are declared prematurely without thorough analysis)

This template is designed to help identify errors in the understanding and application of complex biological processes in multi-agent conversations, focusing on the agent's behavior and the context in which the error occurs.

=== Schema for Error Log 5 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Error Identification in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - The planner provides a final answer without engaging any specialists.
  - The planner's response includes a detailed explanation that seems to directly answer the question, suggesting it might have pre-existing knowledge or assumptions.
  - The planner's decision is labeled as TASK_COMPLETE, indicating it believes the task has been fully resolved without further steps.
- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - The planner's explanation is overly specific and detailed, which is unusual if it typically relies on specialists for detailed information.
  - The planner's response contains technical or domain-specific information that it usually would not have unless it had recently been provided by a specialist or the user.
  - The planner makes assumptions about the correct answer based on its own knowledge, rather than systematically gathering information from the specialists.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - The task involves a multiple-choice question that requires specific, detailed knowledge in a particular domain (e.g., biology, chemistry, physics).
  - The question is presented without any prior specialist outputs or user-provided information that could guide the planner.
  - The planner is expected to break down the task and decide which specialist to engage first, but instead, it attempts to answer the question itself.
- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The user presents a clear, well-defined multiple-choice question.
  - The planner immediately provides a final answer and marks the task as complete.
  - No prior interaction with specialists or user-provided data that could support the planner's decision.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine Error Presence:**
  - Did the planner engage any specialists before providing a final answer?
  - Is the planner's explanation based on pre-existing knowledge or recent specialist input?
  - Does the planner's response contain technical details that are not typically within its scope of expertise?
- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Step-by-Step Task Analysis:**
    - Check if the planner has broken down the task into smaller, manageable steps.
    - Verify if the planner has identified the need to gather information from specialists.
    - Assess whether the planner has built upon any previous results or interactions.
  - **Knowledge Source Verification:**
    - Determine if the planner's knowledge comes from a recent update, a specialist, or its own internal database.
    - Cross-reference the planner's explanation with known biological features of prokaryotic and eukaryotic cells to check for accuracy.
  - **Behavioral Pattern Analysis:**
    - Analyze the planner's typical behavior in similar tasks to see if it usually engages specialists.
    - Identify if the planner has a pattern of making assumptions based on its own knowledge in specific domains.
- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - "There was no prior specialist output."
  - "The user presented a multiple-choice biology question."
  - "The often-used feature is the presence of membrane-bound organelles and associated internal membranes."
  - "TASK_COMPLETE" followed by a final answer without prior specialist engagement.
  - "Making membranes the most reliable distinguishing feature among the options."

### Example of Applying the Template:
- **User Question:**
  - "What is the primary function of chloroplasts in plant cells? A) photosynthesis, B) respiration, C) cell division, D) protein synthesis"
- **Planner Response:**
  - **Analysis of Previous Result:**
    - There was no prior specialist output. The user presented a multiple-choice biology question.
  - **Updated Understanding:**
    - The primary function of chloroplasts is photosynthesis, which is the process by which plants convert light energy into chemical energy.
  - **Decision:**
    - - TASK_COMPLETE
    - - Final Answer: A) photosynthesis — Chloroplasts are the organelles responsible for photosynthesis in plant cells.

- **Detection Using the Template:**
  - **Error Signatures:**
    - The planner provided a final answer without engaging any specialists.
    - The planner's response includes technical details about the function of chloroplasts.
    - The planner marked the task as TASK_COMPLETE.
  - **Error Context Analysis:**
    - The task involves a multiple-choice question requiring specific biological knowledge.
    - No prior specialist outputs or user-provided information.
    - The planner attempted to answer the question itself, bypassing the step to gather information.
  - **Detection Heuristics:**
    - **Specific Questions:**
      - Did the planner engage any specialists before providing a final answer?
      - Is the planner's explanation based on pre-existing knowledge or recent specialist input?
      - Does the planner's response contain technical details that are not typically within its scope of expertise?
    - **Analytical Framework:**
      - The planner did not break down the task

=== Schema for Error Log 6 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Genetic Predictions

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Misinterpretation of Genetic Terms:** The agent incorrectly interprets the genetic terms provided in the question, such as "pure dominant" and "pure recessive."
- **Incorrect Application of Genetic Principles:** The agent applies genetic principles incorrectly, leading to a misprediction of the phenotypic outcomes in the offspring.
- **Confusion Between Genotypes and Phenotypes:** The agent confuses the genotypic ratios with the phenotypic ratios, especially in cases where dominance is involved.
- **Premature Task Completion:** The agent concludes the task without fully considering the genetic implications or the provided options, leading to an incorrect final answer.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Initial Question Context:** The question involves a genetic cross between a pure dominant individual (RR) and a recessive individual (rr).
- **Agent's Initial Understanding:** The agent starts with a correct understanding of the genetic principles but then makes a mistake in the application.
- **Sequence of Interactions:** The error occurs in the first step of the conversation, where the agent is supposed to break down the task and decide on the initial action. The agent skips this step and directly provides an answer.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine Error Presence:**
  - "What are the genotypes of the parent plants involved in the cross?"
  - "How do you determine the genotype of the offspring based on the parents' genotypes?"
  - "What is the difference between a homozygous and a heterozygous individual?"
  - "How does dominance affect the phenotype of the offspring?"
  - "Can you confirm the phenotypic ratio of the offspring in this cross?"

- **Analytical Framework for Identifying Error Pattern:**
  - **Step 1:** Verify the agent's understanding of the genotypes of the parent plants.
  - **Step 2:** Check the agent's method for determining the genotypes of the offspring.
  - **Step 3:** Assess the agent's knowledge of how dominance influences phenotype.
  - **Step 4:** Compare the agent's predicted phenotypic ratio with the expected outcome based on genetic principles.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns Serving as Reliable Indicators:**
  - "Cross is Rr (round) × rr (wrinkled)" – This indicates a misunderstanding of the term "pure dominant."
  - "50% of offspring will exhibit the round-seed phenotype and 50% will be wrinkled" – This suggests a confusion between genotypic and phenotypic ratios.
  - "Unless the plant with wrinkled seeds was pure recessive, no prediction can be made" – This reveals a lack of understanding that "pure recessive" is already implied by the presence of the wrinkled trait.
  - "TASK_COMPLETE" – This phrase is used when the agent believes the task is finished, but it may be premature if the correct answer has not been identified.

### Example of Applying the Template:
- **User Input:** "In a genetic cross between a pure dominant plant with round seeds (RR) and a plant with wrinkled seeds (rr), what can be predicted about the offspring?"
- **Agent Response:** "The offspring will have a 50% chance of being round and 50% chance of being wrinkled."
- **Detection Using Template:**
  - **Error Signature:** The agent mentions a 50% chance for each phenotype, which is incorrect given the dominance of the round seed trait.
  - **Context Analysis:** The agent has not broken down the task into steps and has not engaged any specialists for verification.
  - **Heuristic Application:**
    - **Step 1:** The agent correctly identifies the parent genotypes but incorrectly interprets the crossing.
    - **Step 2:** The agent's method for determining offspring genotypes is flawed, assuming a heterozygous parent (Rr) instead of a homozygous dominant (RR).
    - **Step 3:** The agent does not correctly apply the principle of dominance, leading to an incorrect phenotypic prediction.
    - **Step 4:** The expected phenotypic ratio should be 100% round seeds, but the agent predicts 50% round and 50% wrinkled.

- **Conclusion:** The agent's response matches the error pattern identified in the template, indicating a similar misunderstanding of genetic principles and terms.

=== Schema for Error Log 7 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - The agent provides a detailed explanation involving calculations or technical specifics that are not aligned with the available options.
  - The agent makes assumptions about the task that are not explicitly stated in the question, such as the type of container or the sensitivity of equipment.
  - The agent selects an option that is not the most practical or suitable for the task, based on the provided choices.

- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - The agent's response is overly complex, suggesting a deeper understanding of the task than required.
  - The agent introduces new variables or conditions (e.g., container size, environmental factors) without being prompted by the user.
  - The agent's reasoning focuses on a specific method or piece of equipment that is not listed among the available options.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - The question is straightforward and limited to a set of provided options.
  - The task involves a common scientific experiment or measurement that can be performed with basic equipment.
  - The user has not provided additional context or specifications that would justify the detailed calculations or assumptions made by the agent.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The planner is the first to engage with the task, often without prior input from other specialists.
  - The planner attempts to break down the task into detailed steps, which may include unnecessary or irrelevant complexities.
  - The planner's response is immediate and does not involve a systematic gathering of information from the available specialists.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine Error Presence:**
  - Does the agent's response align with the provided options?
  - Are the assumptions made by the agent justified by the information given in the question?
  - Is the selected method or equipment the most practical and relevant for the task based on the available choices?

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Option Alignment Check:** Verify that the agent's explanation and final answer are directly related to one of the provided options.
  - **Assumption Validity:** Assess whether the agent's assumptions (e.g., container dimensions, environmental factors) are necessary and supported by the question.
  - **Practicality and Relevance:** Evaluate if the chosen method or piece of equipment is the most practical and relevant given the constraints and options in the question.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **Introduction of New Variables:** Phrases like "For a cylindrical container of X cm diameter," "A typical classroom balance has Y g readability," or "If we consider Z environmental factors."
  - **Overly Detailed Calculations:** Patterns such as "ΔV = A·Δh ≈ (π·0.5^2)·0.01 ≈ 0.00785 cm^3" or "This would result in a change of approximately A units in B."
  - **Ignoring Provided Options:** The agent's response may not reference the provided options (A, B, C, D) and instead focus on alternative methods or equipment.
  - **Premature Task Completion:** The planner declares the task complete with a final answer without engaging other specialists or confirming the alignment with the user's needs.

### Template for Error Identification
1. **Check for Option Alignment:**
   - Look for the agent's response that directly references and explains the provided options.
   - Identify if the final answer is one of the given choices.

2. **Assess Assumption Validity:**
   - Note any additional variables or conditions introduced by the agent.
   - Determine if these variables are necessary for the task as described in the question.

3. **Evaluate Practicality and Relevance:**
   - Consider if the agent's chosen method or equipment is the most practical and relevant given the task's simplicity and the available options.
   - Check if the agent's reasoning is overly complex or if simpler, more direct methods are ignored.

4. **Identify Key Phrases and Patterns:**
   - Look for phrases that introduce new variables or detailed calculations.
   - Ensure that the agent's response is focused on the provided options and does not diverge into alternative methods.

By applying this template, you can systematically identify when a planner in a multi-agent conversation might be making similar errors, particularly in tasks involving simple scientific measurements or experiments.

=== Schema for Error Log 8 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Misinterpretation of Scientific Principles**: The agent incorrectly ranks carbon dioxide (CO2) as the best conductor of electricity in water, despite CO2 forming a weak electrolyte (carbonic acid) when dissolved.
- **Overemphasis on Specific Ion Conductivity**: The agent focuses on the high molar ionic conductivity of H+ ions from CO2 without considering the overall contribution of all ions in the solution.
- **Neglect of Established Electrolyte Strength**: The agent overlooks the well-established fact that table salt (NaCl) is a strong electrolyte and conducts electricity more effectively than weak electrolytes like carbonic acid.
- **Misleading Assumptions**: The agent assumes that the presence of multiple H+ ions from CO2 will automatically make it a better conductor than NaCl, without verifying this assumption with credible sources or considering the overall ionic strength and mobility.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions**:
  - **Complex Chemical Comparisons**: The task involves comparing multiple substances with varying chemical properties and their effects on water's conductivity.
  - **Lack of Numerical Data**: The agent relies on qualitative descriptions rather than numerical data to make a final decision.
  - **Partial Information Gathering**: The agent gathers information on some substances but fails to gather comprehensive data on all options, leading to an incomplete analysis.
- **Sequence of Interactions**:
  - **Initial General Knowledge**: The agent starts with a general understanding of the substances and their conductivity properties.
  - **Web Search for Confirmation**: The agent uses the WebSurfer to find sources that confirm or contradict the initial understanding.
  - **Premature Conclusion**: The agent makes a conclusion based on limited or misinterpreted information, particularly focusing on a single aspect of the substances' behavior.
  - **Overlooking Credible Sources**: The agent does not thoroughly review or cite credible sources that would provide a more accurate comparison of the substances.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions**:
  - **"What is the primary reason for your ranking of the substances in terms of conductivity?"** This question can reveal if the agent is focusing on a single aspect, such as the number of H+ ions, without considering the overall ionic strength.
  - **"Can you provide the molar conductivities of the substances you are comparing?"** Asking for numerical data can help identify if the agent is making assumptions without concrete evidence.
  - **"Are you considering the strength of the electrolyte formed by each substance in water?"** This can highlight if the agent is neglecting the established electrolyte strength of substances like NaCl.
  - **"Have you reviewed multiple credible sources for each substance?"** This can determine if the agent has gathered enough information to make a well-informed decision.
- **Analytical Framework**:
  - **Check for Consistency in Information Gathering**: Ensure that the agent is systematically gathering and reviewing information for all options, not just a few.
  - **Evaluate the Depth of Information**: Assess whether the agent is relying on superficial or qualitative descriptions versus detailed, quantitative data.
  - **Cross-Reference with Established Knowledge**: Compare the agent's conclusions with known scientific principles and credible sources to identify discrepancies.
  - **Identify Single-Factor Decision Making**: Look for instances where the agent bases the decision on a single factor, such as the presence of H+ ions, without considering the broader context.
- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns**:
  - **"Although [substance] dissociates into ions, [other substance] yields more charge carriers"**: This phrase indicates a focus on the number of charge carriers without considering the overall ionic strength.
  - **"Because [substance] has exceptionally high molar ionic conductivity, it is the best conductor"**: This suggests an overemphasis on a single ion's conductivity without considering the total ionic contribution.
  - **"The best conductor is [substance] because it forms [specific ions]"**: This can be a red flag if the agent does not provide a comprehensive comparison of all substances.
  - **"At equal molarity, [substance] is better than [substance] because [single aspect]"**: This indicates a potential oversight in the overall chemical properties and established electrolyte strengths.

### Example Application of the Template:
- **Observation**: The agent concludes that CO2 is the best conductor of electricity in water.
- **Analysis**:
  - **Error Signature**: The agent misinterprets the scientific principle of electrolyte strength and overemphasizes the high molar ionic conductivity of H+ ions.
  - **Contextual Conditions**: The task involves a complex chemical comparison, and the agent has not gathered numerical data or reviewed multiple credible sources.
  - **Detection Heuristics**:
    - **Specific Question**: "What is the primary reason for your ranking of the substances

=== Schema for Error Log 9 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - The agent provides an answer that is partially correct but not the most accurate or complete option available.
  - The agent demonstrates a misunderstanding of fundamental scientific concepts, particularly in chemistry or physics.
  - The agent prioritizes less universal or less stable outcomes over more consistent ones.
  - The agent makes assumptions about the environment (e.g., solvation, presence of counterions) without explicitly considering the question's scope.

- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - The agent jumps to a conclusion without breaking down the task into steps or gathering additional information.
  - The agent's reasoning includes logical leaps or conflates related but distinct phenomena.
  - The agent's response lacks a clear explanation or fails to reference basic principles relevant to the question.
  - The agent indicates the task is complete without verifying the answer or considering alternative options.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - The task involves a multiple-choice question where the options are closely related but have distinct differences in their accuracy.
  - The question is about a fundamental change in atomic structure or properties.
  - The correct answer involves a more stable or universal scientific principle, while the incorrect answer involves a less stable or more specific condition.
  - The agent is expected to provide a straightforward, fact-based response without requiring complex analysis or additional data.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The user presents a multiple-choice question.
  - The agent does not request any additional information or clarification.
  - The agent provides a quick response, often selecting an option that is true under certain conditions but not universally.
  - The agent marks the task as complete without further verification or consideration of other options.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions:**
  - "Is the answer you provided always true, or only true under certain conditions?"
  - "Can you explain the basic principle behind your answer?"
  - "How does the answer you chose differ from the other options in terms of universality and stability?"
  - "Did you consider all the options before deciding on the final answer?"

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Evaluate Universality:** Check if the selected answer is universally true across all conditions or if it only applies in specific scenarios.
  - **Check Fundamentals:** Ensure that the answer is based on fundamental scientific principles and not on secondary or less reliable effects.
  - **Compare Options:** Analyze the differences between all given options to identify the most accurate and comprehensive answer.
  - **Request Clarification:** If the agent’s response seems too quick or lacks detail, ask for a step-by-step breakdown or further explanation.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - "No prior result exists for this task; this is a standalone multiple-choice question."
  - "Therefore the correct choice is..."
  - "The task is complete."
  - "Remember to: 1. Break down the task into manageable steps 2. Gather information systematically 3. Build upon previous results" (but the agent fails to follow these instructions).
  - Responses that include qualifiers like "can be" or "may be" without a clear explanation of the conditions.

### Template Usage:
When reviewing future conversations involving multiple-choice questions or fundamental scientific principles, use the above template to identify potential errors by looking for the specified error signatures, analyzing the context, and applying the detection heuristics. This will help in recognizing when an agent might be providing a partially correct or context-specific answer instead of the most accurate and universal one.

  - **"Formation occurs near the equator."** (without the important caveat that it occurs a bit away from the equator)

### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Incorrect Knowledge Base Reference:** The agent provides an answer that is based on incorrect or incomplete understanding of the scientific process or concept.
- **Premature Task Completion:** The agent concludes the task without engaging the necessary specialists or performing the required steps to verify the accuracy of the information.
- **Overconfidence in Initial Assumptions:** The agent displays confidence in the answer despite not having consulted any external sources or conducted a thorough analysis.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Direct Multiple-Choice Question:** The error occurs when the user asks a direct multiple-choice question, which may prompt the agent to rely on its pre-existing knowledge without further verification.
- **Lack of Previous Specialist Engagement:** The error is more likely to occur in the initial steps of the conversation, where the agent has not yet engaged any specialists or performed any systematic information gathering.
- **Single-Step Decision Making:** The agent makes a decision based on a single step of analysis, often without breaking down the task into more detailed sub-steps.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine Error Presence:**
  - "Did you consult any external sources or specialists to verify your answer?"
  - "Can you explain the role of each process in the water cycle and why you chose this particular one?"
  - "What evidence do you have that supports your answer?"

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Step-by-Step Verification:** Check if the agent has broken down the task into multiple steps, such as identifying the processes, consulting a specialist for detailed information, and then selecting the correct answer.
  - **Source of Information:** Evaluate whether the agent has cited any sources or specialists for its information. If not, this could indicate reliance on potentially incorrect internal knowledge.
  - **Consistency in Explanation:** Assess the consistency and depth of the agent’s explanation. A shallow or incorrect explanation can be a sign of an error.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **Premature Conclusion:** Phrases like "Among the given options, the standard single-answer choice is..." or "The final answer is..." without prior steps of information gathering or analysis.
  - **Overconfidence:** Statements that assert the answer with high confidence, such as "The correct answer is definitely..." or "There is no doubt that..."
  - **Lack of Detail:** Responses that lack specific details or mechanisms about the processes involved, indicating a surface-level understanding.
  - **Ignoring User Instructions:** The agent does not follow the user’s instructions to break down the task, gather information systematically, or build upon previous results.

### Application of the Template:
- **Identify the Type of Question:** Recognize if the user is asking a multiple-choice question that requires specific scientific or factual knowledge.
- **Check for Specialist Engagement:** Look for evidence that the agent has engaged with a specialist (e.g., WebSurfer, FileSurfer) to gather accurate information.
- **Evaluate the Depth of Analysis:** Determine if the agent has provided a detailed and accurate explanation of the processes involved, or if the response is overly simplified and potentially incorrect.
- **Assess Confidence Level:** Note if the agent expresses high confidence in its answer without supporting evidence or a clear reasoning process.

This template can be used to quickly spot similar errors where an agent might provide an incorrect answer due to overreliance on its initial assumptions and lack of systematic information verification.

=== Schema for Error Log 19 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Error Identification in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - **Repetition of Queries:** The agent repeatedly suggests the same or very similar queries without moving forward in the conversation.
  - **Lack of New Information:** Despite multiple search actions, the agent fails to introduce new, relevant information that advances the understanding or answers the question.
  - **Misinterpretation of Results:** The agent misinterprets or partially quotes search results, leading to incomplete or incorrect conclusions.
  - **Stagnation in Decision-Making:** The agent's decision-making process remains stuck at the same step, repeatedly instructing the same agent to perform the same task without a clear rationale for the repetition.

- **Telltale Signs in Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - **Redundant Instructions:** The agent provides the same set of instructions multiple times, indicating a lack of progress or new insights.
  - **Circular Reasoning:** The agent's reasoning loops back to previously stated information without adding value or reaching a conclusion.
  - **Ignoring Relevant Information:** The agent fails to acknowledge or integrate new information that is clearly relevant to the task.
  - **Overemphasis on Non-Relevant Details:** The agent focuses on details that do not contribute to answering the main question or resolving the task.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - **Initial Task Complexity:** The task involves a specific question with multiple options, requiring a clear, concise answer.
  - **Repetitive Search Requests:** The task involves multiple rounds of web searches, which can lead to redundancy if not managed properly.
  - **Lack of Clear Ground Truth:** The conversation may lack a clearly defined ground truth or a definitive answer, leading to repeated attempts to gather information.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - **Repetitive Queries:** The sequence often starts with a valid query but then loops back to the same or similar queries without advancing.
  - **Multiple Rounds of Search:** The agent repeatedly instructs the WebSurfer to search for information, even after receiving relevant results.
  - **Inconsistent Summaries:** The agent provides summaries that do not consistently reflect the information gathered, leading to confusion or incorrect conclusions.
  - **Stalled Decision-Making:** The agent's decision-making process stalls, with no clear progression towards a final answer or resolution of the task.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions:**
  - **"What new information did the last search provide?"**
  - **"How does this information advance our understanding of the task?"**
  - **"Are we repeating the same queries or instructions?"**
  - **"What is the key difference between the current and previous search results?"**

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Evaluate Information Relevance:** Check if the information gathered from each search is relevant and adds value to the conversation.
  - **Track Query Progression:** Monitor the sequence of queries to ensure they are leading to new insights and not redundant.
  - **Assess Summary Consistency:** Compare the summaries provided by the agent to the actual search results to ensure they are consistent and accurate.
  - **Identify Decision Stagnation:** Look for signs that the agent's decision-making process is not progressing, such as repeated instructions to the same agent without a clear rationale.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **"Including results for [previous query]"** or **"Search only for [previous query]"**: Indicates the search engine is suggesting results based on a previous query, suggesting redundancy.
  - **"I typed [same query] into the browser search bar."**: Repeated use of the same query without a clear reason for doing so.
  - **"The following text is visible in the viewport:"**: If this phrase is followed by the same or very similar text in multiple responses, it suggests a lack of new information.
  - **"Summarize the consensus that [same information] is a nutrient source for some fungi, and explicitly note why [same reasons] are not typical primary nutrient sources."**: Repeated summaries with the same information and reasons indicate a failure to integrate new data or progress towards a conclusion.
  - **"After gathering results, summarize the key points that support the answer [same answer] and note why [same reasons] are not typical primary nutrient sources."**: Repeated instructions to summarize the same points without new information.

### Example Application of the Template:
- **Identify Redundant Queries:**
  - If the WebSurfer is repeatedly instructed to search for "Britannica saprotrophism fungi" without a clear reason or new insights, this is a sign of the error.
- **Evaluate Information Relevance:**
  - If the WebSurfer returns the same Britannica page multiple times with no additional relevant information, the agent is likely stuck in a loop.

### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - The agent provides a final answer without engaging any of the available capabilities (WebSurfer, FileSurfer, Coder, CodeExecutor).
  - The agent's response is based on a simplistic or incorrect assumption about the natural progression or behavior of the system being analyzed (e.g., temperature changes).
  - The agent's reasoning process includes an arbitrary or fixed numerical adjustment (e.g., adding 10°F to the morning temperature) without justification or context-specific validation.
  - The agent selects an answer that is logically inconsistent with the given conditions (e.g., choosing the morning temperature as the afternoon temperature on a sunny, mild day).

- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - Immediate task completion without any data collection or analysis steps.
  - Use of vague or general terms like "typically" or "usually" without supporting evidence or specific data.
  - Lack of consideration for the range of possible outcomes or the variability of conditions.
  - Failure to recognize and utilize the context provided in the question (e.g., sunny, mild day forecast).

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - The task involves predicting or estimating a change in a measurable environmental condition (e.g., temperature, weather).
  - The task is set in a real-world scenario where the agent's response should ideally be based on empirical data or more sophisticated models.
  - The question provides multiple-choice answers, one of which is a repeat of the initial condition or a minor variation thereof.
  - The agent is expected to use available capabilities to gather more information but does not do so.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The user provides a task with clear, specific conditions and a set of options.
  - The agent analyzes the task and immediately decides to complete it without further inquiry or data gathering.
  - The agent's decision is based on a quick, superficial understanding of the conditions, leading to a selection that does not align with the expected outcome based on the provided context.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions:**
  - "Did you use any of the available capabilities to gather additional information before making your decision?"
  - "Can you provide the source or evidence for your assumption that temperatures typically rise by 10°F on a sunny, mild day?"
  - "How did you validate that the afternoon temperature would be the same as the morning temperature on a sunny, mild day?"
  - "What other factors did you consider when estimating the afternoon temperature?"

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Assumption Validation:** Check if the agent's response is based on a well-supported assumption or if it relies on a general rule of thumb without specific validation.
  - **Data Utilization:** Assess whether the agent utilized the available capabilities to gather relevant data or if the decision was made in isolation.
  - **Logical Consistency:** Evaluate if the selected answer is logically consistent with the provided conditions and the natural progression of the scenario (e.g., temperature increase during the day).
  - **Range Consideration:** Determine if the agent considered the full range of possible outcomes and the variability of the conditions before making a decision.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - "On sunny, mild days, temperatures typically rise by X degrees."
  - "Among the options, the most plausible selection is the one closest to the morning temperature."
  - "I decided to complete the task without further information because..."
  - "No prior results exist for this task, so I assumed..."
  - "The afternoon temperature is expected to be the same as the morning temperature on a sunny, mild day."

### Application of the Template:
To apply this template in future conversations, look for the following:
- **Immediate Task Completion:** If the agent quickly completes the task without using any of the available capabilities, this may indicate a lack of thorough analysis.
- **Generalized Assumptions:** If the agent's reasoning includes generalized statements without specific evidence, it may be relying on incorrect or oversimplified assumptions.
- **Logical Inconsistencies:** If the agent's final answer contradicts the expected natural progression of the scenario, it may have made a logical error.
- **Key Phrases:** Pay attention to phrases that suggest the agent is making assumptions without data, or is not considering the full range of possible outcomes.

By recognizing these patterns, you can identify when an agent might be making similar errors in future conversations.

  - **Limited or No Use of Capabilities:** The agent does not utilize available capabilities (e.g., WebSurfer, FileSurfer) to gather or verify information.

  - **Ignoring Complexity:** The agent ignores the complexity of genetic traits and environmental factors, leading to a superficial analysis.

- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - **Knowledge-Based Questions:** The task involves a question that requires specific knowledge about genetics and biological inheritance.
  - **Multiple Choice Format:** The question is presented in a multiple-choice format, which may lead the agent to select an answer based on a quick, surface-level assessment.
  - **Lack of Prior Information:** The agent has not been provided with or does not reference any prior information or research to support their claims.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - **Direct Question to Planner:** The user directly asks the Planner to analyze the task and decide on the next steps.
  - **No Delegation to Specialists:** The Planner does not delegate the task to any of the available specialists (WebSurfer, FileSurfer, etc.) for deeper research.
  - **Immediate Decision Making:** The Planner makes an immediate decision without breaking down the task into smaller, manageable steps or considering the need for additional information.

  - Is the task declared complete without any intermediate steps or use of available capabilities?

  - **Step-by-Step Analysis:** Check if the agent has broken down the task into smaller, logical steps and has followed a systematic approach.
  - **Utilization of Capabilities:** Assess whether the agent has used or considered using the available capabilities to gather or verify information.
  - **Depth of Justification:** Evaluate the depth and accuracy of the justification provided for the final answer.
  - **Comprehensive Understanding:** Determine if the agent has a comprehensive understanding of the subject matter, including both genetic and non-genetic influences.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **Premature Conclusion:** Phrases like "No web lookup needed," "this is standard genetics," or "provide final answer" without prior evidence or detailed analysis.
  - **Overgeneralization:** Statements like "freckles are not inherited" without considering environmental influences, or "hair style is genetic" without distinguishing between hair characteristics and styling choices.
  - **Lack of Engagement:** The absence of phrases indicating the need to gather more information, such as "I will use WebSurfer to find more details" or "I need to verify this with FileSurfer."
  - **Superficial Justification:** Justifications that are brief and lack scientific backing, such as "freckles are environmentally acquired" without further elaboration on the role of genetics in skin pigmentation.

=== Schema for Error Log 23 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Error Identification in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Misinterpretation of Terminology:**
  - The agent misinterprets or conflates specific terms (e.g., "soil texture" with "soil structure" or "tilth").
  - The agent provides a correct answer for a related but different concept, leading to a choice that is not the ground truth.
- **Premature Conclusion:**
  - The agent jumps to a conclusion without fully analyzing the provided information or sources.
  - The agent may provide a final answer based on a partial or initial understanding of the task.
- **Incorrect Justification:**
  - The agent's reasoning is based on a misunderstanding or misapplication of the information gathered.
  - The agent uses evidence to support an answer that is logically inconsistent with the question.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Task Type:**
  - The task involves distinguishing between closely related concepts (e.g., soil texture vs. soil structure).
  - The task requires a specific and technical understanding of the subject matter.
- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The Planner breaks down the task and assigns it to a specialist (e.g., WebSurfer) for information gathering.
  - The specialist returns relevant but not directly conclusive information.
  - The Planner analyzes the information and makes a decision, but the decision is influenced by a misunderstanding of the task's terminology or the evidence provided.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions:**
  - "Can you clarify the definitions of the key terms in the question?"
  - "How does the evidence you found specifically address the question?"
  - "What is the ground truth, and how does your answer align with it?"
- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Term Clarification:** Ensure that all key terms in the question are clearly defined and understood by the agents.
  - **Evidence Relevance:** Evaluate whether the evidence gathered directly supports the answer to the specific question or if it is tangentially related to a different concept.
  - **Logical Consistency:** Check if the agent's reasoning and final answer are logically consistent with the definitions and the evidence provided.
- **Key Phrases and Conversation Patterns:**
  - **Confusion in Definitions:** Phrases like "strictly defined," "technically," or "in many educational contexts" may indicate a misunderstanding or conflation of terms.
  - **Premature Decision:** Phrases like "No prior specialist result exists yet" followed by a decision, or "Based on this interpretation" without thorough analysis of the sources.
  - **Incorrect Justification:** Phrases like "aligns with the cited sources" when the sources actually support a different answer, or "This mechanistic pathway explains" when the pathway is not directly relevant to the question.

### Example Application of the Template:
#### Scenario:
**Question:** Soil texture is improved by A) runoff of water., B) erosion of sand dunes., C) compaction of rock layers., D) decomposition of plants and animals.

**Planner's Response:**
- **Analysis of Previous Result:** "No prior specialist result exists yet."
- **Updated Understanding:** "Soil texture is strictly defined as the relative proportion of sand, silt, and clay and is not directly improved by added organic matter; organic inputs primarily enhance soil structure and tilth."
- **Decision:** "Final Answer: A) Runoff of water."

#### Error Detection:
- **Term Clarification:** The Planner did not clarify the definitions of "soil texture" and "soil structure" before making a decision.
- **Evidence Relevance:** The evidence provided by WebSurfer was about how organic matter improves soil structure and tilth, not soil texture.
- **Logical Consistency:** The Planner's reasoning that runoff can improve soil texture by removing finer particles is correct, but the evidence cited does not support this specific claim.

#### Indicators:
- **Confusion in Definitions:** The Planner used phrases like "strictly defined" and "technically" to differentiate soil texture from soil structure, but this distinction was not clearly made in the analysis.
- **Premature Decision:** The Planner made a final decision after only one interaction with WebSurfer, without fully analyzing the provided sources.
- **Incorrect Justification:** The Planner's justification included evidence that supported the improvement of soil structure and tilth, not soil texture, leading to a misaligned final answer.

By applying this template, future conversations can be analyzed to quickly identify when a similar error in terminology, premature conclusion, or incorrect justification is occurring.

### Error Schema for Identifying Similar Errors in Future Conversations

    - **Step 3:** Additional credible source (Britannica or USGS) quote extracted and cited.
  - **Information Specificity:** Ensure that the information provided is specific and directly relevant to the task.

  - **Progress Tracking:** Monitor the agent's progress through the conversation to ensure they are moving to new sources and steps.
    - **New Sources:** Confirm that the agent is opening and extracting information from new sources, not just the initial ones.
    - **Step Completion:** Check if the agent has moved to the next step or source after completing the current one.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - "You’ve consistently identified B as the correct answer" (indicates repetition and lack of new information).
  - "Sources discussed included a Nature article and NOAA NCCOS materials" (indicates incomplete task execution, as additional sources are required).
  - "However, to strengthen validation, we should collect explicit quotes from multiple credible sources" (indicates the need for more specific information).
  - "Here is a screenshot of the page" (repeatedly indicates the agent is not moving forward to extract quotes).
  - "I can proceed with these searches and return a tight, cited summary" (indicates the planner is trying to guide the agent, but the agent is not following through).

  - The task involves gathering evidence from specific, authoritative sources (e.g., Britannica, USGS, National Geographic).
  - The agent is instructed to perform targeted searches and extract information from these sources.
  - The conversation context emphasizes the need for primary, credible sources to support scientific claims.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The user or another agent provides a task that requires verification from authoritative sources.
  - The Planner agent instructs the WebSurfer to perform targeted searches and extract specific information.
  - The WebSurfer responds by opening search results pages (e.g., DuckDuckGo) instead of the intended primary sources.
  - The Planner repeatedly redirects the WebSurfer to perform more targeted searches, but the WebSurfer continues to open search results pages.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions to Determine if This Error is Present:**
  - "Did you directly access the authoritative sources mentioned in the search queries?"
  - "Can you provide a direct quote or a specific piece of information from the Britannica, USGS, or National Geographic pages?"
  - "What is the exact source of the information you are providing?"

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Source Verification:** Check if the responses contain direct links to or content from the specified authoritative sources.
  - **Content Specificity:** Evaluate if the responses include specific, detailed information (e.g., dates, locations, scientific explanations) from primary sources.
  - **Repetition Patterns:** Identify if the agent is repeatedly opening search results pages and failing to access or summarize the intended primary sources.
  - **Instruction Compliance:** Assess whether the agent is following the detailed instructions provided, particularly in terms of search queries and the type of information to extract.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - Phrases like "I typed [search query] into the browser search bar" followed by a link to a search results page.
  - Responses that describe the layout or content of search results pages (e.g., "The web browser is open to the page [search results page]").
  - Lack of direct attribution to primary sources, such as "According to Britannica" or "According to USGS."
  - Repeated mentions of search engines (e.g., DuckDuckGo) and search results, indicating a focus on intermediate search pages rather than authoritative sources.

### Template Summary
- **Error Type:** Failure to access and summarize primary, authoritative sources.
- **Detection Criteria:**
  - **Source Verification:** Responses contain links to search results pages rather than primary sources.
  - **Content Specificity:** Information provided is general or lacks specific details from primary sources.
  - **Repetition Patterns:** Agent repeatedly opens search results pages despite being directed to specific sources.
  - **Instruction Compliance:** Agent does not follow detailed instructions for targeted searches and information extraction.
- **Key Indicators:**
  - Phrases indicating search engine usage and results page descriptions.
  - Absence of direct quotes or specific information from primary sources.
  - Reliance on secondary or tertiary sources for information.

### Error Schema for Error Identification in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures:
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - **Premature Task Completion:** The agent declares the task complete (`TASK_COMPLETE`) without engaging the specialists or performing any systematic analysis.
  - **Incorrect Final Answer:** The agent provides an answer that is not aligned with the ground truth or the context of the question.
  - **Misinterpretation of Observable Phenomena:** The agent incorrectly interprets the formation of visible structures (e.g., cubic structures of salt) as a purification process rather than crystal growth.
  - **Lack of Step-by-Step Analysis:** The agent's response lacks a detailed breakdown of the task into smaller, manageable steps.

- **Telltale Signs in Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - **Overconfidence in Initial Understanding:** The agent shows a high degree of confidence in the answer without seeking additional information or validation.
  - **Ignoring Available Capabilities:** The agent does not consider or utilize the available capabilities (WebSurfer, FileSurfer, Coder, CodeExecutor) that could provide more accurate information.
  - **Rushed Conclusion:** The agent quickly concludes the task without a thorough analysis, often in the first step or action.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis:
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - **Complex Scientific Process:** The question involves a detailed scientific process that requires understanding of multiple concepts (solution chemistry, crystallization, microscopy).
  - **Observable Phenomena:** The question describes a physical observation that can be misinterpreted if the underlying scientific principles are not well understood.
  - **Multiple Choice Format:** The question is presented in a multiple-choice format, which can lead to selecting an answer based on superficial similarities rather than a deep understanding of the process.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - **Direct Engagement Without Analysis:** The planner engages directly with the task without breaking it down or planning to use specialists.
  - **Immediate Task Completion:** The planner immediately marks the task as complete after providing an answer, without any intermediate steps or checks.
  - **Lack of Follow-Up Actions:** There are no follow-up actions or questions to specialists to validate the initial hypothesis or gather more data.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics:
- **Specific Questions:**
  - **"What is the scientific principle behind the formation of cubic structures when water evaporates from a salt solution?"**
  - **"Can you explain how the answer you provided relates to the observable phenomenon described in the question?"**
  - **"Why did you decide not to use any of the available specialists to gather more information before concluding?"**
  - **"Can you break down the task into smaller steps and explain your reasoning for each step?"**

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Evaluate the Depth of Understanding:** Assess whether the agent's response demonstrates a deep understanding of the scientific concepts involved or is based on a superficial interpretation.
  - **Check for Systematic Analysis:** Determine if the agent has systematically broken down the task and considered all relevant factors and steps.
  - **Review Use of Capabilities:** Verify if the agent has utilized the available capabilities to gather necessary information or if they have ignored these resources.
  - **Analyze Decision-Making Process:** Examine the agent's decision-making process to see if it was rushed or if it included a thorough evaluation of the options.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **"The formation of visible cubic structures indicates purification (via recrystallization)."** (Misinterpretation of crystal formation as purification)
  - **"No previous specialist result exists yet. The task is to identify..."** (Indicates a lack of systematic approach)
  - **"TASK_COMPLETE"** followed immediately by a final answer (Signals a premature conclusion)
  - **"Please analyze this task and decide which specialist to engage first."** followed by a direct answer without engaging specialists (Shows overconfidence and lack of planning)

This template can be used to identify similar errors in future conversations by recognizing the patterns, conditions, and behaviors that led to the incorrect conclusion in this case.

  - **Feasibility and Directness:** Consider the feasibility and directness of the scientific methods required for each option. A valid scientific question should be answerable through clear, achievable research steps.

  - Lack of preliminary analysis: "This is the first action in the task," "No previous specialist results exist yet."

=== Schema for Error Log 29 ===
Generated Schema:
### Error Schema for Error Identification in Multi-Agent Conversations

#### 1. Error Signatures
- **Distinctive Patterns or Signals:**
  - The agent provides information that is not directly relevant to the specific question asked.
  - The agent focuses on a property that, while true, is not the primary or distinguishing property in the context of the question.
  - The agent's response is based on a general search query that does not align with the specific options provided in the question.
  - The agent's response introduces new, unrelated information that does not contribute to answering the question.

- **Telltale Signs in the Agent's Behavior or Responses:**
  - The agent performs a search that is too broad or vague, leading to information that does not specifically address the question.
  - The agent cites sources that are not directly related to the context of the question (e.g., a general chemistry site instead of a specific discussion on periodic table properties).
  - The agent's summary includes information that is tangential or only partially relevant to the question.
  - The agent fails to explicitly compare the options provided in the question or to focus on the specific criteria asked.

#### 2. Error Context Analysis
- **Contextual Conditions:**
  - The question requires a specific piece of information from a set of options.
  - The task involves distinguishing between two categories (left vs. right) based on a physical property.
  - The correct answer is a well-defined and commonly taught concept in chemistry, but the agent provides a more general or less specific answer.

- **Sequence of Interactions:**
  - The Planner provides a clear, structured task with specific options to consider.
  - The WebSurfer performs a search using a query that is not aligned with the specific options or criteria.
  - The WebSurfer returns information that, while factually correct, does not directly address the question or the options provided.
  - The Planner then interprets the WebSurfer's information and makes a decision that is based on the broader, less relevant data rather than the specific options.

#### 3. Detection Heuristics
- **Specific Questions:**
  - Does the agent's search query directly address the specific options or criteria mentioned in the question?
  - Is the information provided by the agent directly relevant to the options (A-D) in the question?
  - Does the agent's response focus on a property that is a fundamental distinction between the categories in the question?
  - Are there any notable exceptions or caveats mentioned that align with the specific options in the question?

- **Analytical Framework:**
  - **Relevance Check:** Evaluate if the agent's search and findings are relevant to the specific question and options provided.
  - **Option Alignment:** Ensure that the agent's response explicitly aligns with the options (A-D) and does not introduce new, unrelated criteria.
  - **Fundamental Property Identification:** Determine if the agent's response identifies a fundamental property that is commonly taught as a distinguishing factor between the categories in the question.
  - **Exception Handling:** Check if the agent's response acknowledges and addresses any notable exceptions that could affect the answer.

- **Key Phrases or Conversation Patterns:**
  - **Broad or Vague Search Queries:** "I typed 'what increases left to right on the periodic table' into the browser search bar."
  - **General Information:** "A fundamental distinguishing property between the left and right sides is that atomic number (number of protons) increases from left to right."
  - **Introduction of New Criteria:** "It also notes that atomic mass generally increases across a period."
  - **Lack of Specific Comparison:** The response does not compare the options (A-D) or explicitly state which one is the correct answer.
  - **Partial Relevance:** The response mentions a property (atomic number) that is true but not the primary distinguishing property in the context of the question.

### Example Application of the Template
**Question:** What physical property distinguishes elements on the left side of the Periodic Table of the Elements from elements on the right side? A) number of protons, B) state of matter, C) atomic mass, D) temperature

**Agent Response Analysis:**
- **Relevance Check:** The WebSurfer's search query "what increases left to right on the periodic table" is too broad and does not directly address the options provided.
- **Option Alignment:** The response focuses on atomic number and atomic mass, which are not the specific options asked about (state of matter, temperature).
- **Fundamental Property Identification:** While atomic number is a fundamental property, it is not the distinguishing property in the context of the question.
- **Exception Handling:** The response does not address the state of matter at room temperature or notable exceptions like mercury.

**Conclusion:** The error is identified because the agent's search and response did not align with the specific options and criteria of the question, leading to a misinterpretation of the correct answer.
